- **T10-B trusted offline-sync guard — 2026-10-15:** Isolated `codex/t10-runtime-sync` after `9dbcaff`; exact checkpoint is the commit containing this entry. ### Historical development log (retained; not current acceptance)

- **2026-10-11:** Product Chinese display name is confirmed as **Honsen采购管理系统**; `HONSEN Procure` is the English companion name. The GitHub repository was renamed to `etianwang/Honsen-procure`. This changes product-facing documentation and repository naming only, not the unapproved Toolbox identity values or local credential-service name.
- **Historical implementation summary corrected:** T00/T01/T03/T05/T06/T07 are accepted; T02/T04/T08/T09/T10 still have outstanding acceptance. See the authoritative snapshot above and [docs/BUILD-TASKS.md](docs/BUILD-TASKS.md).
- **2026-10-10:** Product owner approved the initial immutable-code mapping. `docs/MASTER-DATA-CODE-APPROVAL.md` now records countries, projects, sites, departments, teams, units and the CI 18% tax template. These remain controlled deployment data, never hard-coded migration seeds.
- **2026-10-10:** T04 now rejects attempts to change an existing country/project/site/department/team/unit/tax-template business code. Both the PostgreSQL store and local SQLite/Rust boundary return structured immutable-code errors before mutation; this prevents a same-UUID upsert from silently reporting an unpersisted code.
- **2026-10-10:** T04 local trusted commands now persist tax templates and append exchange-rate records only for an authorized session. They enforce supported currencies, integer basis points, date/rate syntax and a mandatory reason for a manual rate; Rust tests cover permitted persistence and manual-rate rejection without a reason.
- **2026-10-10:** The project-default screen is now a reusable editor over a typed Tauri adapter, not a fixture-only form. The Rust boundary lists local active projects/defaults and saves through the trusted command; the browser test injects the Tauri adapter and verifies navigation, retrieval and save feedback.
- **2026-10-10:** Local tax/exchange date validation now accepts only real calendar dates in exact `YYYY-MM-DD` form, including leap-year February; the regression test rejects `2026-02-30` before any SQLite write.
- **2026-10-10:** The Fastify master-data API test now covers authenticated tax-template save, manual exchange-rate rejection without a reason, and accepted manual-rate creation. No local PostgreSQL runtime is installed, so integration migration tests remain intentionally skipped rather than using an unknown `.env` connection.
- **2026-10-10:** CI now provides an isolated PostgreSQL 16 service to make migration tests a mandatory cloud gate. `compose.test.yaml` and `docs/POSTGRES-INTEGRATION-TESTING.md` provide the equivalent disposable local path; neither uses deployment configuration.
- **2026-10-10:** WSL PostgreSQL testing revealed Vitest was discovering generated `dist/**/*.test.js` as a duplicate test suite, causing concurrent migration setup. Server tests now need a source-only Vitest include rule before PostgreSQL evidence can be accepted.
- **2026-10-10:** Server startup, migration and initial-super-administrator provisioning now perform a bounded read-only PostgreSQL readiness probe (250 ms to 4 s backoff) for hosted Neon cold starts. The probe retries only connection/startup failures; no purchase, approval or other business mutation is automatically replayed.
- **2026-10-10:** The production server bootstrap now shares one configured PostgreSQL pool across authentication and master-data services, so authenticated master-data routes are present outside test fixtures as well. Pool shutdown follows Fastify shutdown.
- **2026-10-10:** T05 now has immutable SQLite migration `0004_local_catalog` for materials, suppliers, append-only price history and material-image source/crop metadata. Purchaser/administrator Rust commands can save materials/suppliers and append a price; an engineer is denied writes. A replacement quote deactivates the previous active quote in one SQLite transaction rather than rewriting history.
- **2026-10-10:** The material overview now reads via a typed `search_catalog` Tauri adapter. It displays up to four non-blacklisted active supplier quotes; the browser fixture uses explicitly synthetic supplier records and verifies engineer-only search/read behavior.
- **2026-10-10:** T05 material maintenance now has a purchaser/admin modal for create, edit and soft-deactivate. The DOM test fills all required fields and asserts that `save_material` reaches the Tauri adapter; an Ant Design pre-mount warning was fixed with a pre-rendered modal form.
- **2026-10-10:** Trusted local catalog access now exposes an authenticated `list_suppliers` command with full supplier maintenance fields. The typed frontend adapter is ready for supplier and price-maintenance UI.
- **2026-10-10:** T05 is complete. Purchasers/administrators can create/edit/deactivate materials and suppliers, append supplier-specific price history, crop material images to a square and persist both original/crop locally. Engineers/viewers remain read-only. Local catalog image crop accepts browser-decodable PNG/JPEG/WebP; HEIC remains supported for T11 attachments but is not browser-cropped in T05.
- **2026-10-10:** T06 began with immutable local migration `0006_local_requisitions.sql`. It adds parent-requisition headers, material/supplier snapshot line storage, exact-integer total fields and attachment metadata; it deliberately has no outbox or cloud upload behavior before T10/T11.
- **2026-10-10:** T06 shared contracts now define requisition headers, material/supplier line snapshots, fixed/percent adjustments and exact `BigInt` totals. Tests lock the calculation order to line discount → order discount → freight → tax, including half-up percentage/tax rounding and excessive-discount rejection.
- **2026-10-10:** Neon free-tier cold-start behavior is now explicit: startup-only read-only readiness checks wait up to about 7.75 seconds, exceeding the expected roughly three-second wake-up. The same change fixed a T06 history gap: each requisition line now snapshots both its discount and freight rule; freight is allowed to exceed the goods value while discounts are capped at their calculation base.
- **2026-10-10:** T06 now exposes the parent-requisition vertical slice: a purchaser can select a local material, enter two-decimal quantity, discount and freight, add/remove rows, receive exact shared-calculator totals, autosave a valid draft locally and submit it as `pending_confirmation`. The Rust boundary derives material/supplier snapshots from SQLite instead of accepting renderer display values.
- **2026-10-10:** T07 is in progress. SQLite migration `0007_order_workflow` introduces forward-compatible workflow status, rejection/confirmation fields, immutable comment storage and permanent order audit storage without rewriting existing 0006 orders. The trusted Rust boundary now denies default self-approval, requires a non-empty rejection reason, permits an applicant or privileged purchaser/admin to add an immutable comment, and audits confirmation/rejection atomically. A Tauri-backed approval panel blocks blank rejection reasons and makes sent comments visibly immutable; `@` is preserved as text until the sync-backed notification task exists.
- **2026-10-10:** T07 comment history is persistent: `list_order_comments` reads ordered immutable records through the same authenticated Rust boundary, and the approval panel loads that history before allowing a new comment. Playwright proves a pre-existing Tauri-returned comment renders on first load.
- **2026-10-11:** T07 now supports a controlled confirmed-order revision: only a purchaser/administrator can revise a `confirmed` order, must provide a reason, cannot transfer the applicant, and leaves its workflow state as `confirmed`. Rust snapshots relevant header and line values before/after the mutation into permanent audit rows; `list_order_audits` lets the applicant read those rows. The approval panel renders changed fields in red. Direct edits to sent/closed orders remain rejected by the same state guard.
- **2026-10-11:** The product owner authorized read-only use of the ignored root `.env` Neon and COS runtime configuration for real connectivity tests. Never display, log, copy to fixtures or commit these values; never write, migrate, delete or deploy through those credentials without a separate explicit authorization. Use separate production credentials and rotate test credentials before release instead of deleting live configuration.
- **2026-10-11:** T07 now has immutable migration `0008_approval_policy.sql`. `allow_self_approval` defaults to off; only a super administrator can change it with a required reason and a permanent policy-audit row. When enabled, purchaser/administrator self-confirmation is explicitly marked on the order and uses a separate audit action. The policy panel is a typed Tauri adapter fixture; Rust remains the authorization boundary.
- **2026-10-11:** T07 comment mentions now create local persistent notifications through immutable migration `0009_order_notifications.sql`. Only exact `@username` references to enabled cached accounts are notified; the author is excluded, duplicate mentions collapse to one recipient, and email addresses are ignored. The comment response reports the notification count so the UI can confirm delivery intent without exposing recipient details.
- **2026-10-11:** T08 now has immutable migrations `0010_supplier_child_orders.sql` and `0011_order_item_currency.sql`. Every historic order item is backfilled from its order currency and every new line persists its currency snapshot. A purchaser/administrator can allocate every item of a confirmed parent exactly once into child orders keyed by supplier and currency; each child snapshots the supplier, locks a non-zero decimal exchange rate, resets supplier-order adjustments for manual completion, and leaves immutable allocation/audit evidence. A parent with children cannot be revised, preventing destructive replacement of referenced parent items. Child allocation UI, RFQ, multi-currency parent entry and locked-rate primary aggregation remain next.
- **2026-10-11:** T08 now exposes a keyboard-accessible supplier-allocation workbench through a typed Tauri adapter. It requires a supplier, child number and locked rate for every parent line, emits all allocation records in one request, and lists the child records returned by the trusted boundary. The `?test=allocation` fixture is only DOM evidence; production routing still needs confirmed-parent list/detail data.
- **2026-10-11:** T08 now permits parent lines with different source currencies. A mixed parent carries source-currency totals only until allocation; its order-level tax, discount and freight are forcibly deferred to child supplier orders, so no invalid cross-currency arithmetic is stored. Locked-rate conversion uses integer rational arithmetic across XOF's zero and CNY/USD/EUR's two minor digits; it updates the parent primary-currency total during allocation. The DOM regression uses keyboard selection for Ant Design's virtualized currency options.
- **2026-10-11:** T08 now includes price-free RFQ creation. `0012_rfqs.sql` persists supplier snapshots plus source material/quantity/unit snapshots; only a purchaser/administrator may create an RFQ from a confirmed internal parent and an active, non-blacklisted supplier. A trusted command copies only the selected-currency lines in one transaction and writes an order audit entry. Any RFQ prevents a destructive confirmed-parent revision. The typed React fixture proves the Tauri payload excludes pricing.
- **2026-10-11:** T08 supplier children now require a purchaser/administrator to save the complete child terms before sending: every allocated line has its own discount/freight/included-freight snapshot, while the child has order discount, order freight and tax rate. `0014_supplier_child_terms.sql` records that completion. Exact integer arithmetic recalculates every stored amount and writes before/after audit snapshots; only then can the child enter `sent`. The DOM test validates the save-before-send guard, and Rust tests cover calculated terms, sending and receipt states.
- **2026-10-11:** T09 receipt data now links pre-validated order attachment records to the immutable receipt batch through `0015_receipt_attachment_links.sql`. The trusted boundary rejects duplicate or wrong-order links and permits at most ten image attachments per receipt. Actual local file ingestion, image compression, byte-budget validation and COS upload remain T11 responsibilities so the renderer never submits arbitrary filesystem paths.
- **2026-10-11:** T10 service-side sync foundation now has shared bounded Push/Pull contracts, authenticated Fastify endpoints, immutable PostgreSQL migration `003_sync.sql`, idempotent event acknowledgements, optimistic-version conflict records and cursor-based change-log reads. Engineers can only push their own bound-project requisitions or receipts; purchasers/administrators can write business data, and viewer accounts cannot push. The desktop SQLite transactional outbox, durable Pull application, resolver UI and WebSocket hint delivery remain required before T10 is complete.
- **2026-10-11:** T10 desktop outbox begins with immutable local migration `0016_local_sync_outbox.sql`. Material, supplier and append-only material-price mutations now enqueue a full serialized payload in the same SQLite transaction as their business write. Pending events calculate their expected base version from the last acknowledged server version plus earlier queued events for that entity, avoiding an avoidable self-conflict during ordered offline retries. Order/workflow/RFQ/receipt/master-data write paths still need the same treatment before T10 can be completed.
- **2026-10-11:** The same outbox now covers parent purchase-order draft saves and submit/confirm/reject transitions. The serialized order event contains its immutable line snapshot plus applicant, project and workflow status, so server-side engineer project scope can be verified without trusting renderer fields. Supplier-child allocation/terms/send, RFQ, receipts, comments and master-data mutations remain to be queued.
- **2026-10-11:** Supplier-child terms and supplier-send transitions now also enqueue the child-order snapshot atomically. Supplier-child allocation, RFQ, receipt and close mutations are still pending outbox coverage.
- **2026-10-11:** Receipt registration now atomically queues a receipt event (with project and receiver scope) and the post-receipt supplier-child order snapshot. Supplier-child allocation, RFQ and conditional-close mutations still require coverage.
- **2026-10-11:** Supplier-child allocation now queues both the updated parent and every generated child order. RFQ creation queues the RFQ plus its source parent snapshot. Conditional-close, comments and master-data write paths still require outbox coverage.
- **2026-10-11:** Conditional supplier-child close now queues its immutable post-close order snapshot atomically with the status/audit write. Comments and master-data mutations still require outbox coverage.
- **2026-10-11:** T10 now exposes authenticated local outbox batch reads (maximum 100) and transactional application of server Push acknowledgements. Only `accepted` events are acknowledged; conflict/validation responses remain pending for a resolver. A repeated older acceptance is idempotent and cannot reduce the stored server version. HTTPS transport and Pull apply are still pending.
- **2026-10-11:** T10 Push now uses a Rust-only HTTPS client with a bounded 20-second timeout. The base URL is deployment-time `HONSEN_SYNC_API_BASE_URL`, accepted only as a clean HTTPS URL; the authenticated session token is stored in OS credential storage and is never persisted in SQLite or exposed by a Tauri command. `0017_local_sync_state.sql` creates a non-secret installation UUID and persistent sync-state row. Pull application, conflict UI and WebSocket hints remain pending.
- **2026-10-11:** `0018_local_sync_remote_entities.sql` and the trusted Pull command now receive a bounded HTTPS cursor page, stage all clean remote entities and advance its cursor in one SQLite transaction. A pending local outbox event creates immutable conflict evidence instead of being overwritten. Clean material, supplier and material-price pulls are now schema-validated and projected to local catalog tables inside the same transaction; tombstones soft-deactivate the local row. Other entity types remain safely staged until their explicit projectors are implemented. The Rust regression verifies a remotely pulled supplier quote is searchable and a later dirty local material is not overwritten.
- **2026-10-11:** Project print defaults, all six reference master-data kinds, tax templates and exchange rates now write their local SQLite record and serialized outbox event in one transaction. Invalid writes and immutable-code rejections produce no event. The Rust regression verifies that each supported master-data entity type is pending after a valid administrative write.
- **2026-10-11:** Pull projection now validates and applies project defaults, country/project/site/department/team/unit, tax templates and exchange rates to local SQLite in the same transaction as staging and cursor movement. Master-data tombstones soft-deactivate records; immutable exchange-rate history gained migration `0019_local_exchange_rate_tombstones.sql` so a remote tombstone remains auditable. The regression covers dependent country/project/team records, tax/default settings, an exchange rate and its tombstone.
- **2026-10-11:** Immutable order comments now enqueue their own `order_comment` sync event with order/project/author evidence, and clean pulled comments are inserted without permitting overwrite or deletion. The server independently limits an engineer comment to a project-bound order whose already-synced applicant matches the authenticated author; comment tombstones are rejected for every role.
- **2026-10-11:** Local sync conflicts now retain the remote operation and support trusted administrator/super-administrator resolution for directly projectable catalog, master-data and immutable-comment records. A chosen local value supersedes the rejected outbox event and creates a new versioned event; a chosen server value is projected in the same transaction. Purchase-order conflicts remain deliberately excluded from direct overwrite. Migrations `0020` and `0021` preserve the difference between superseded events and cloud acknowledgements, and preserve remote tombstones for resolution.
- **2026-10-11:** The desktop now has a reusable accessible sync-conflict panel over the typed Tauri sync adapter. It requires a reason, offers explicit local/server resolution actions and removes the resolved row only after the trusted command returns. The query-route fixture is DOM evidence only; production role/session routing will mount the same component for administrator and super-administrator workspaces.
- **2026-10-11:** T10 now exposes an authenticated `/api/sync/ws` WebSocket endpoint using the Fastify official plugin. An accepted Push broadcasts only a `dataset-changed` hint; it carries no entity payload or attachment data and clients must still use cursor Pull for authoritative state. The endpoint is registered in its own Fastify plugin scope (so a real upgrade works), and an integration test opens an authenticated socket, pushes an accepted event and verifies the minimal hint.
- **2026-10-11:** Desktop Pull now follows every full 500-change page in the same authenticated synchronization run. It rejects a malformed non-advancing/non-empty or cursor-advancing/empty server page before altering the durable local cursor; the regression covers continuation, tail-page stop and malformed empty-page rejection.
- **2026-10-11:** The three cloud migrations were applied successfully to a newly created disposable WSL PostgreSQL database and the four T10 sync tables were verified. Node/PostgreSQL integration suites remain skipped in the Windows local gate because Docker is unavailable and the WSL instance is not exposed on the Windows test port; CI provides its own isolated PostgreSQL service and will execute them there. No Neon or root `.env` credentials were read.
- **2026-10-11:** The product's English companion name is now **HONSEN Procure**. GitHub source repository was renamed from `etianwang/Honsen-materials` to `etianwang/Honsen-procure`; local display documentation, UI labels, package metadata and Toolbox source-repository field are aligned. Toolbox identity fields remain unassigned and unchanged.
- Product, security, data, sync, UI and Toolbox integration specifications have been written and reconciled.
- A temporary React/Vite renderer exists at `apps/desktop` to validate the agreed Chinese visual direction. It contains a Home workbench and a Materials overview only; it is **not** yet a shippable Tauri desktop application. T03 now provides a tested Rust/SQLite authentication core, but it is deliberately not connected to release packaging.
- The renderer uses Ant Design and Lucide. Current visible records are explicitly demo data and must never be treated as real purchasing data.

## Fixed issues — do not regress

| ID      | Symptom/cause                                                                                                                                                          | Fix                                                                                                                                               | Regression prevention                                                                                                                   |
| ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| BUG-001 | Conversation inline visualization failed with `Inline visualization failed while running`.                                                                             | Replaced the visualization approach with static HTML visual drafts; no runtime script is required for those drafts.                               | Keep visual specification artifacts static unless an interaction is specifically required and tested.                                   |
| BUG-002 | Ant Design emitted a console warning because `Button.iconPosition` is deprecated.                                                                                      | Replaced it with `iconPlacement` in `HomePage.tsx`.                                                                                               | Treat browser-console warnings as failures during each task’s DOM test.                                                                 |
| BUG-003 | Documents disagreed on minor currencies: one database note said EUR while requirements said USD.                                                                       | Reconciled support to XOF/CNY/USD/EUR across requirements and data documentation.                                                                 | Currency enum, integer minor-unit helper and all migrations must share one tested source of truth in T01.                               |
| BUG-004 | Specifications assumed separate device-enrollment credentials after the business decision removed them.                                                                | Replaced with authenticated account-session authorization and non-secret installation tracking.                                                   | Do not add `/api/devices/enroll`, device tokens or public registration; test account disable/session revocation instead.                |
| BUG-005 | The first Playwright navigation test found an ambiguous accessible name for “材料库”; an Ant Design `Alert.message` deprecation also reached the browser console.      | Targeted exact accessible names for sidebar buttons and changed the Alert property to `title`.                                                    | DOM tests must assert unique accessible controls and fail on browser-console errors.                                                    |
| BUG-006 | The T03 DOM test removed the native `disabled` attribute, but Ant Design still absorbed the click, so it did not prove a tampered material-edit request was rejected.  | Captured the attempted control activation and surfaced the explicit denial state; the Rust boundary separately tests the real authorization rule. | Keep DOM tamper proof and Rust authorization tests together; a disabled UI control alone is never proof of security.                    |
| BUG-007 | The project-default DOM test targeted the Ant Design native `option`, while the visible popup uses separate elements; the test waited for a non-clickable node.        | Target the visible popup item and assert a dedicated selected-project output.                                                                     | For Ant Design selects, test the visible popup interaction and an explicit persisted/selected state rather than the hidden input value. |
| BUG-008 | With a PostgreSQL test URL, Vitest discovered generated `dist/**/*.test.js` as a second suite and raced migration creation.                                            | Restricted the server Vitest include rule to `src/**/*.test.ts`.                                                                                  | Keep generated output outside test discovery.                                                                                           |
| BUG-010 | Catalog search synchronously reset state inside a React effect, producing a lint warning about cascading renders.                                                      | Let the async query completion update result/error state instead.                                                                                 | Treat zero lint warnings as part of the task quality gate.                                                                              |
| BUG-011 | Material form values were set before the Ant Design modal form mounted, causing a browser-console warning.                                                             | Pre-render the modal form so its instance is connected before population.                                                                         | Playwright console assertions must cover modal-opening flows.                                                                           |
| BUG-012 | Pre-rendering every maintenance modal made hidden form labels collide with visible labels in screen-reader queries.                                                    | Populate forms after each dialog mounts instead of force-rendering every dialog.                                                                  | Use unique accessible control roles/names in nested dialog DOM tests.                                                                   |
| BUG-013 | Ant Design's virtualized select can expose duplicate option text or leave a text option non-clickable in Playwright.                                                   | Select the active currency with the combobox keyboard path (`ArrowDown`, `Enter`).                                                                | Prefer keyboard interactions for virtualized select options after the combobox receives focus.                                          |
| BUG-014 | Two overlapping Playwright processes raced while deleting/archiving the shared `test-results` directory, producing ENOENT trace errors after otherwise-complete tests. | Re-ran the full suite with one worker after the overlapping run ended.                                                                            | Do not run two Playwright sessions against the same results directory concurrently.                                                     |
| BUG-009 | The catalog browser fixture used heterogeneous tuple values, so TypeScript widened supplier fields to `string \| boolean`.                                             | Replaced tuples with named quote objects before mapping.                                                                                          | Keep fixture records structurally typed; never suppress a boundary type failure.                                                        |

Tax/exchange audit writes are transactional, and a hand-entered exchange rate requires a non-empty reason at the shared validation boundary.
- Local migration `0003_reference_master_data.sql` now mirrors the country/project/site/department/team/unit/tax/exchange entities with foreign keys, soft-delete/version fields and currency/reason constraints. It runs after `0001` and `0002` with the same checksum guard.

## Non-negotiable implementation rules

1. Desktop is Tauri 2 + React + TypeScript + SQLite; cloud is TypeScript HTTPS API + PostgreSQL. Desktop never connects to PostgreSQL.
2. Every application launch requires account password plus local CAPTCHA; enabled cached accounts may sign in offline for at most 24 hours.
3. React is untrusted. Rust/Tauri and server independently authorize every protected mutation.
4. SQLite mutation plus outbox event is one transaction. HTTPS Push/Pull is authoritative; WebSocket is only a Pull hint.
5. Saved order lines snapshot all catalog/supplier/price/tax data. Catalog changes never rewrite historic orders.
6. Use UUIDs, soft deletion, versions, idempotency and explicit conflicts. Never silently overwrite conflicts.
7. Comments are immutable after sending. Closed orders are immutable. Sent supplier orders require cancellation/revision, never direct edit.
8. Do not invent Toolbox app identity or release settings. Internal automatic updates still require the exact Toolbox/Runner contract.
9. Visible UI strings need complete `zh-CN`, `fr-FR` and `en-US` catalogs; current Chinese-only demo strings must be replaced during T13.

## Known limits and risks — current interpretation

- Frontend permission helpers remain visibility-only. Rust/server authorization now exists for implemented boundaries; synthetic DOM fixtures and unmapped production routes are not end-to-end security acceptance.
- Ant Design raises the initial renderer bundle to roughly 183 KB gzip. Defer route-level code splitting until there are real routes; do not prematurely fragment the current demo.
- Toolbox packaging/update work remains blocked by a permanent `appId`, install directory, EXE name, installer choice and internal update URL.
- COS/PostgreSQL values are intentionally not known to the codebase. Use runtime environment injection and never copy `.env` values to desktop code or tests.
